I believe the divorce really affected him, which caused him to drink more.</p><p><br></p><p>He would rip up my story books and flush them down the toilet, call me names like idiot, prostitute etc. Well you know what they say, the apple does not fall far from the tree, here I am in adulthood - an alcoholic myself. My dad became mean and although not directly emotionally abusive, just really mean. I believe the divorce really affected him, which caused him to drink more.</p><p><br></p><p>He would rip up my story books and flush them down the toilet, call me names like idiot, prostitute etc. Well you know what they say, the apple does not fall far from the tree, here I am in adulthood - an alcoholic myself. I'd like to think of myself as a functioning alcoholic because I have 2 kids, two good jobs, pay all my bills on time etc. However, I drink everyday, without fail. Whether I'm sick, alone, happy, unhappy. I drink. The only difference is I am not  mean, you probably would not even know unless you saw it for yourself. I think me holding up my image comes from seeing how embarrassing it was to see people like my dad who could not and let him self, self-destruct. </p><p><br></p><p>My dad was a good dad for the most part and the most loving when he was not drunk, but that was hardly ever as he always did. Whether I'm sick, alone, happy, unhappy. I drink. The only difference is I am not  mean, you probably would not even know unless you saw it for yourself. I think me holding up my image comes from seeing how embarrassing it was to see people like my dad who could not and let him self, self-destruct. </p><p><br></p><p>My dad was a good dad for the most part and the most loving when he was not drunk, but that was hardly ever as he always did. I'd like to think of myself as a functioning alcoholic because I have 2 kids, two good jobs, pay all my bills on time etc. However, I drink everyday, without fail. Whether I'm sick, alone, happy, unhappy. I drink. The only difference is I am not  mean, you probably would not even know unless you saw it for yourself. I think me holding up my image comes from seeing how embarrassing it was to see people like my dad who could not and let him self, self-destruct. </p><p><br></p><p>My dad was a good dad for the most part and the most loving when he was not drunk, but that was hardly ever as he always did. He raised me, took me to school, made all my meals, home taught me as well, did what good parent do.</p><p><br></p><p>One day in adulthood, I went through a rough homelessness patch and needed to stay at my dads until i was back on my feet. Has been all my life and although I was used too it, it very much bothered me. As we all know, most alcoholics harm people not only emotional but sometimes physical. He used to get physical with my mum and have really bad fights. This caused her to leave him when I was 4. S he left me with him and I stayed with him until the age of 12, where I then went to leave with her. This was there arrangement. My dad became mean and although not directly emotionally abusive, just really mean. I believe the divorce really affected him, which caused him to drink more.</p><p><br></p><p>He would rip up my story books and flush them down the toilet, call me names like idiot, prostitute etc. Well you know what they say, the apple does not fall far from the tree, here I am in adulthood - an alcoholic myself. Whether I'm sick, alone, happy, unhappy. I drink. The only difference is I am not  mean, you probably would not even know unless you saw it for yourself. I think me holding up my image comes from seeing how embarrassing it was to see people like my dad who could not and let him self, self-destruct. </p><p><br></p><p>My dad was a good dad for the most part and the most loving when he was not drunk, but that was hardly ever as he always did.
